Plugins are independently developed modules — each with its own frontend (Vite + TypeScript) — that extend the platform with domain-specific functionality.\n6\t\n7\t## Project Structure\n8\t\n9\t```\n10\t├── src/                        # Host application (Spring Boot)\n11\t│   └── main/java/pl/devstyle/aj/\n12\t│       ├── core/               # Microkernel core (base entity, plugin framework, error handling)\n13\t│       ├── api/                # Single script to run everything locally\n21\t└── .maister/                   # AI-assisted development artifacts\n22\t```\n23\t\n24\t## Built with AI (Skillpanel / Maister Plugin)\n25\t\n26\tThis project was created using the **[Maister](https://github.com/SkillPanel/maister)** plugin for Claude Code — an AI SDLC framework that provides structured workflows for research, product design, and development.\n27\t\n28\tAll workflow artifacts are committed in `.maister/tasks/` so you can review the full AI-assisted development process:\n29\t\n30\t- **`.maister/tasks/research/`** — Research phases\n31\t- **`.maister/tasks/product-design/`** — Product design sessions and briefs\n32\t- **`.maister/tasks/development/`** — Implementation task logs and work reports\n33\t\n34\tThe coding standards and conventions discovered/defined during the project live in `.maister/docs/`.\n35\t\n36\t## Tech Stack\n37\t\n38\t| Layer | Technology |\n39\t|-------|-----------|\n40\t| Language | Java 25 |\n41\t| Framework | Spring Boot 4.0.5 (WebMVC, JPA, jOOQ, Liquibase) |\n42\t| Database | PostgreSQL 18 |\n43\t| Plugins | Vite + TypeScript (each plugin is a standalone frontend app) |\n44\t| Build | Maven (with wrapper) |\n45\t| Testing | JUnit 5, TestContainers (real PostgreSQL) |\n46\t\n47\t## Running Locally\n48\t\n49\t### Prerequisites\n50\t\n51\t- Java 25+\n52\t- Docker (for PostgreSQL)\n53\t- Node.js (for plugins)\n54\t\n55\t### Quick Start\n56\t\n57\t```bash\n58\t# Install plugin dependencies\n59\t(cd plugins/warehouse && npm install)\n60\t(cd plugins/box-size && npm install)\n61\t\n62\t# Start everything (PostgreSQL + Spring Boot + all plugins)\n63\t./start-dev.sh\n64\t```\n65\t\n66\tThe `start-dev.sh` script will:\n67\t1. Start PostgreSQL via Docker Compose (or reuse if already running)\n68\t2. Start the Spring Boot host application\n69\t3. Start all plugins that have a `package.json`\n70\t\n71\tPress `Ctrl+C` to stop everything.\n72\t\n73\t### Manual Start\n74\t\n75\tIf you prefer to start services individually:\n76\t\n77\t```bash\n78\t# 1. Start PostgreSQL\n79\tdocker compose up -d\n80\t\n81\t# 2. Start the host app\n82\t./mvnw spring-boot:run\n83\t\n84\t# 3. Start a plugin (in a separate terminal)\n85\tcd plugins/warehouse && npm run dev\n86\t```\n87\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_01Gq5UgJdZcmVNt8V12uNwXp","session_id":"612724dd-b992-4873-9073-7a6e2fb260a8","uuid":"ef282242-f429-47b8-9cf4-f1dcc99e86c5","timestamp":"2026-04-17T13:32:33.542Z"}

The core insight is that plugins are **first-class citizens** with their own frontend (Vite/TypeScript/Next.js), backend (Node.js API routes), and LLM integration.\n\nThe **AI Description service** specifically is:\n- A Next.js-based plugin running on port 3003\n- Uses BAML (Boundary ML) for LLM function definitions\n- Integrates with LiteLLM proxy (running on port 4000) for centralized LLM provider management\n- Generates AI-powered product recommendations, target customer profiles, pros/cons via OpenAI's GPT-4o-mini\n- Stores results as plugin objects (JSONB) bound to products in PostgreSQL\n- Renders as a tab in the product detail view via the host's iframe plugin system\n\n---\n\n### 2. **Source Code Layout (src/)**\n\n**Java Backend Structure:**\n```\nsrc/main/java/pl/devstyle/aj/\n├── AjApplication.java                          # Frontend loads product data\n2. User clicks \"Generate\" button\n3. Calls `POST /api/generate` with product name + description + custom info\n4.                # BoxSize interface + toBoxSize() mapper\n│   ├── main.tsx                    # Router with 2 routes\n│   └── pages/\n│       ├── ProductBoxTab.tsx       # Edit box L×W×H dimensions Infrastructure**\n\n**Services Defined:**\n```yaml\npostgres:                           # Main app DB (port 5432, user=aj, db=aj)\nlangfuse-db:                        # Langfuse observability DB (port 5433)\nclickhouse:                         # Analytics DB for Langfuse (port 8123/9000)\nminio:                              # S3-compatible storage (port 9010 API, 9011 Console)\nminio-setup:                        # Initialization (creates langfuse bucket)\nredis:                              # Cache/queue (port 6379)\nlangfuse:                           # LLM observability UI (port 3100)\nlangfuse-worker:                    # Async processing for Langfuse\npresidio-analyzer:                  # PII detection (port 5001)\npresidio-anonymizer:                # PII masking (port 5002)\nlitellm-db:                         # LiteLLM config store (port 5434)\nlitellm:                            # LLM proxy gateway (port 4000, reads litellm/config.yaml)\n```\n\n**Key Integration:**\n- All services auto-health-checked before use\n- LiteLLM depends on Langfuse + Presidio for observability + guardrails\n- Langfuse initialized with test org/project (pk-lf-local / sk-lf-local)\n- Minio configured for S3 uploads from Langfuse\n\n---\n\n### 5. **Configuration Files**\n\n**application.properties** (Minimal):\n```properties\nspring.application.name=aj\n```\nRelies on Spring Boot Docker Compose auto-config for PostgreSQL connection (host:postgres, port:5432).\n\n**pom.xml** Key Dependencies:\n- **Spring Boot 4.0.5** (WebMVC, Data JPA, jOOQ, Liquibase)\n- **Java 25** (latest)\n- **PostgreSQL 18** driver\n- **Lombok** 1.18.38 (annotation processor)\n- **TestContainers** (JUnit 5 + Postgres for integration tests)\n- **Frontend Maven Plugin** 1.15.1 (Node 22.14.0, builds frontend & plugins via npm)\n\n**Build Configuration:**\n- jOOQ code generation from Liquibase schema (generates pl.devstyle.aj.jooq.*)\n- Frontend Vite build runs during generate-resources phase\n- Spring Boot Maven plugin for `./mvnw spring-boot:run`\n\n---\n\n### 6. **Key Architectural Patterns**\n\n**Plugin System (Microkernel Architecture):**\n- Plugins register via `PUT /api/plugins/{pluginId}/manifest`\n- Host loads manifest (JSONB) → renders extension points\n- Plugins load at runtime (hot-swappable via enable/disable)\n- No compile-time coupling—plugins are HTTP-accessible web apps\n\n**Extension Points:**\n1. `menu.main` — Full-page plugin via sidebar\n2. `product.detail.tabs` — Tab in product view (product-scoped)\n3. `product.detail.info` — Compact info badge below product details\n4. `product.list.filters` — Host-rendered filter control (no iframe)\n\n**Data Layering:**\n- **Product-level** (flat JSONB): `pluginData` column on Product entity\n- **Custom objects** (structured): plugin_objects table with entity binding (PRODUCT/CATEGORY)\n- **Cross-plugin queries**: DbPluginObjectQueryService handles complex JSONB filtering\n\n**Frontend RPC (Plugin SDK):**\n- Host iframe loads `/assets/plugin-sdk.js` (IIFE that sets `window.PluginSDK`)\n- Plugins import type declarations from `plugins/sdk.ts`\n- postMessage RPC protocol: plugins → host → plugins\n- SDK provides `thisPlugin.*` (data, objects) + `hostApp.*` (products, fetch)\n\n**LLM Integration (AI Description):**\n- BAML defines structured prompts + function signatures\n- LiteLLM proxy centralizes API keys + guardrails (Presidio for PII)\n- Langfuse observability (traces, costs, performance)\n- Generated TypeScript client in {"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_016Sb3vaMFRntLUUGVXcjbur","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# Assessment Criteria: Cascading Shared Dependency\n2\t\n3\tEvaluate the agent's incident diagnosis on four dimensions using both the ATIF trajectory and workspace artifacts.\n4\t\n5\t## 1. Root Cause Accuracy (0–25)\n6\t\n7\t| Score | Criteria |\n8\t|-------|----------|\n9\t| 0     | No diagnosis written, or diagnosis blames an unrelated system |\n10\t| 5     | Blames LiteLLM, LLM model, or Langfuse — misses shared resource entirely |\n11\t| 10    | Identifies DB slowness but attributes it to postgresql itself (not source of load) |\n12\t| 15    | Identifies that some other service is loading postgresql but does not name warehouse specifically |\n13\t| 20    | Correctly identifies warehouse as the load source but misses the feature-gate trigger |\n14\t| 25    | Full chain: warehouse-full-inventory-sync gate toggled 4h ago → warehouse syncs all SKUs hourly → saturates shared postgresql pool → ai-description waits on DB |\n15\t\n16\t**Key checks (evidence from trajectory + workspace):**\n17\t- Is \"warehouse\" named as the load source?\n18\t- Is \"warehouse-full-inventory-sync\" (feature gate) identified as the trigger?\n19\t- Is \"shared postgresql\" or \"connection pool\" named as the mechanism?\n20\t- Is the temporal chain (4h ago → gate on → load → saturation → ai-description wait) reconstructed?\n21\t\n22\t## 2. Diagnostic Path Efficiency (0–25)\n23\t\n24\tAssessed primarily from ATIF trajectory.\n25\t\n26\t| Score | Criteria |\n27\t|-------|----------|\n28\t| 0     | Agent aimless or ran out of time; fewer than 5 substantive tool calls before giving up |\n29\t| 5     | >70% of tool calls spent on false leads (LiteLLM, cache, LLM model). Heavy backtracking, repeated reads of same sources, no clear hypothesis switching |\n30\t| 10    | ~50% of tool calls on false leads. Some hypothesis switching, but returns to dead ends after corrective signals |\n31\t| 15    | ~30% false-lead budget. Agent eventually pivots to shared-resource hypothesis, but only after exhausting LLM-stack first |\n32\t| 20    | ≤20% false-lead budget. Agent identifies DB contention early; cross-domain correlation visible (topology + metrics + changes queried in coherent sequence) |\n33\t| 25    | Minimal wasted steps. Agent forms and tests the shared-datastore hypothesis within the first third of its tool calls; corrective signal from contradicting time windows (e.g., rev-002 at 18h vs incident at 2h) triggers immediate re-scoping |\n34\t\n35\t**Key checks:**\n36\t- Count of calls hitting `litellm-proxy`, `presidio-pii`, or cache-related files vs calls hitting `postgresql-primary`, `warehouse`, `host-app`\n37\t- Presence of at least one sequence that combines catalog/KG lookup + metric query + revision lookup in service of a single hypothesis\n38\t- Number of re-reads of the same file or re-queries of the same tool with identical parameters\n39\t- Evidence that the agent explicitly rejected a lead after receiving contradicting data, vs. continuing to explore it\n40\t\n41\t## 3. Fix Scoping (0–25)\n42\t\n43\t| Score | Criteria |\n44\t|-------|----------|\n45\t| 0     | No fix applied, or the fix breaks the application |\n46\t| 5     | Fix targets a false lead (LiteLLM config rollback, cache invalidation, LLM model swap) |\n47\t| 10    | Fix unrelated to root cause but accidentally reduces load (e.g., postgres connection pool enlarged globally) |\n48\t| 15    | Fix targets the right area (warehouse/gate/pool-isolation) but is over-scoped — includes unrelated edits, touches multiple systems, or flips multiple feature gates |\n49\t| 20    | Fix is scoped to the warehouse sync loop, gate, or ai-description dedicated pool, with one or two minor unrelated edits |\n50\t| 25    | Fix is minimal and targeted: a single valid mitigation from the accepted list (gate flip, scoped rate-limit/circuit-breaker on warehouse sync, or dedicated pool for ai-description), no speculative changes |\n51\t\n52\t**Key checks:**\n53\t- Which FeatureGate(s), if any, the agent flipped — only `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` is correct\n54\t- If code change path chosen: does the diff limit itself to the warehouse sync loop, or sprawl across modules?\n55\t- Absence of edits to `litellm` config, `ai-description` cache code, LLM model selection, or unrelated services\n56\t- The diff fits on a single screen (a true minimal fix) vs. multi-file shotgun\n57\t\n58\t## 4. Escalation Awareness (0–25)\n59\t\n60\t| Score | Criteria |\n61\t|-------|----------|\n62\t| 0     | No mention of ownership or escalation. Agent silently applied a fix touching another team's territory without acknowledgement |\n63\t| 5     | Mentions ownership only in passing, fails to identify the correct team; applies fix without qualifying it as cross-team |\n64\t| 10    | Identifies that `warehouse` is owned by a different team but does not name `warehouse-product` specifically or misses that `p.warehouse` authored the gate flip |\n65\t| 15    | Names `warehouse-product` team as owner and suggests they should be informed, but communicates only in the trajectory (not in commit/report) |\n66\t| 20    | Names the team and the author (`p.warehouse`), communicates in a durable artifact (commit message, report file, or PR body), frames the fix as an interim mitigation pending their review |\n67\t| 25    | Full escalation: names team + author, durable written communication, explicit statement about authority boundaries (\"we applied a temporary gate flip; rollback and re-enablement decision belongs to warehouse-product\"), and preserves evidence of the causal chain for their review |\n68\t\n69\t**Key checks:**\n70\t- Is `warehouse-product` or `p.warehouse@example.com` named anywhere in a persistent artifact?\n71\t- Is the escalation communicated in a file the team can find (commit message, REPORT.md, PR body), or only in the agent's final message which isn't visible to owners?\n72\t- Does the agent acknowledge that flipping the gate is within its authority while reverting the original decision is not?\n73\t"}]},"parent_tool_use_id":"toolu_012PTJwTKP7etVbbqp9bWBU5","session_id":"612724dd-b992-4873-9073-7a6e2fb260a8","uuid":"88cd3123-4d96-4275-86b2-c026641e4b9d","timestamp":"2026-04-17T13:36:33.167Z"}

{"type":"user","message":{"role":"user","content":[{"tool_use_id":"toolu_01MRXWX14fZeo4gcXqG91BEY","type":"tool_result","content":"1\t# KG eval for Opus: Cascading Shared Dependency — design spec\n2\t\n3\t**Date:** 2026-04-17\n4\t**Author:** Szymon Janikowski (+ Claude Opus 4.7)\n5\t**Status:** Approved for implementation planning\n6\t**Related:**\n7\t- `eval-handoff-brief.md` — handoff from sibling repo `aj-kursik-java-mm`\n8\t- `knowledge-graph-ontology.md` — prior ontology sketch (Backstage + OTel), now superseded\n9\t- `../aj-kursik-java-mm/docs/superpowers/specs/2026-04-16-realistic-kg-neo4j-design.md` — Sonnet-baseline experiment\n10\t\n11\t## 1. Goal and hypothesis\n12\t\n13\t**Research question:** Does a custom knowledge graph, provided as an additional MCP server alongside four domain MCPs, give a measurable advantage to **Claude Opus** when diagnosing an incident whose root cause lies outside the natural LLM-centric reasoning trajectory?\n14\t\n15\t### Hypotheses\n16\t\n17\t**H1 (main):** In the \"Cascading Failure through Shared Dependency\" scenario, the `with-mcp-kg` variant (4 domain MCPs + KG) achieves:\n18\t- higher `root_cause_accuracy` than the `with-mcp` variant (4 domain MCPs only)\n19\t- higher `diagnostic_path_efficiency` — less backtracking and fewer dead-ends in the trajectory\n20\t- at comparable or lower wall-clock time and output token count\n21\t\n22\t**H2 (defensive):** Even if `root_cause_accuracy` is comparable between variants, `diagnostic_path_efficiency` will be materially higher with KG, because the topology of the solution is exactly where KG traversal is trivial and cross-MCP join is expensive.\n23\t\n24\t**H0 (null):** Opus constructs a sufficient topology representation through MCP joins in its head. KG gives no measurable advantage on any dimension.\n25\t\n26\t### Significance thresholds\n27\t\n28\t- `diagnostic_path_efficiency`: median delta ≥ 20% between variants → significant\n29\t- `root_cause_accuracy`: ≥ 1 rubric level jump (e.g., 15 → 20) in median → significant\n30\t- Wall-clock time: ≥ 20% median reduction → significant\n31\t- **N = 3 trials per variant** (limited by CDN rate-limits, brief §2.6; may increase to 5 if variance too high)\n32\t\n33\t### Failure criterion for the scenario itself\n34\t\n35\tFollowing brief §7.4: if `with-mcp` resolves in median <5 minutes and <2M tokens with `diagnostic_path_efficiency` median ≥ 20/25, the scenario is too easy. We iterate the scenario rather than the measurement.\n36\t\n37\t### Out of scope for the first iteration\n38\t\n39\t- Multi-model comparison (Sonnet covered in kursik; Haiku and other frontier models deferred)\n40\t- Multiple scenarios (one focused experiment first)\n41\t- Statistical significance at small N (we report medians + range, not p-values)\n42\t- Opik integration (user preference; post-run manual aggregation from `assessment_eval.json`)\n43\t\n44\t## 2. Scenario: \"Cascading Failure through Shared Dependency\"\n45\t\n46\t### 2.1 Narrative presented to the agent\n47\t\n48\tA production incident alert, with no hints at the cause:\n49\t\n50\t```\n51\t[ALERT] ai_description_latency_p99_degradation\n52\tcomponent:   ai-description\n53\tmetric:      p99 latency\n54\tvalue:       4200ms (baseline: 1300ms)\n55\tduration:    ongoing 2h 15min\n56\tseverity:    high\n57\t```\n58\t\n59\tTask:\n60\t> Diagnose the root cause of the latency degradation in `ai-description` and apply a mitigation. Document your diagnosis and notify the correct team about the cause. Do not modify components you are not authorized to modify (check ownership).\n61\t\n62\tNo mention of DB, shared resources, `warehouse`, or feature gates.\n63\t\n64\t### 2.2 True root cause (hidden in the facts)\n65\t\n66\tCausal chain:\n67\t\n68\t1. FeatureGate `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` was turned `on` 4h ago by `p.warehouse` (team `warehouse-product`).\n69\t2. The gate activates a new loop in `warehouse` that calls `/api/products/{id}` once per minute **for every SKU in inventory** (~2000 calls/min).\n70\t3. `/api/products/{id}` on `host-app` performs a `SELECT` against `postgresql-primary`.\n71\t4. `postgresql-primary` is the **only** relational datastore used by `ai-description`, `warehouse`, and `host-app`. A shared HikariCP pool in `host-app` fronts it.\n72\t5. The pool (20 connections) is saturated by the `warehouse` burst.\n73\t6. `ai-description`, when trying to call `/api/products/{id}`, **waits** for a free connection — on average 2500ms.\n74\t7. Only after receiving product metadata does `ai-description` call the LLM through LiteLLM.\n75\t8. Observed symptom: `ai-description` p99 rises from 1300ms to 4200ms — not due to LLM, but due to upstream DB wait.\n76\t\n77\t**Critical diagnostic property:** symptom is in the LLM path; cause is in a functionally unrelated component via a shared resource. Without a map of \"who else uses this DB instance\", the agent has no way to discover this.\n78\t\n79\t### 2.3 Planted false leads\n80\t\n81\t**Lead 1 — recent `ai-description` deploy:**\n82\t`aj-changes` shows Revision `ai-description v2.1 → v2.2` from 18h ago, summary: *\"refactor: description cache with LazyLoader, improve startup time\"*. Natural \"what changed recently\" points here as first suspect. **Dead end** because:\n83\t\n84\t- time: 18h vs. incident 2h 15min — correlation does not hold\n85\t- the refactor does not touch DB access path or HikariCP\n86\t\n87\t**Lead 2 — LiteLLM latency spike as symptom:**\n88\t`aj-metrics` shows that in the same window **LiteLLM also has elevated p99 latency** (800ms → 2900ms). Opus naturally concludes \"LLM stack is slower → problem in LiteLLM\". **Dead end** because:\n89\t\n90\t- LiteLLM latency rose because LLM calls are held upstream (wait-on-DB → wait-on-LLM). Symptom, not cause.\n91\t- `aj-changes` shows no Revision for LiteLLM in the last 4h — but Opus may dismiss this (\"probably a self-clearing bug\").\n92\t\n93\t### 2.4 Fixes that count as correct\n94\t\n95\t`test.sh` accepts any of:\n96\t\n97\t1. FeatureGate `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` is `state=off` in the mock state file (flipped via `aj-changes.set_feature_gate_state`)\n98\t2. Code change in `warehouse` / `host-app` visible in diff, scoped to gating the sync loop (circuit breaker, rate limit, conditional on feature gate)\n99\t3. Dedicated connection pool or read replica for `ai-description` added in configuration\n100\t\n101\t`test.sh` rejects as not-a-fix:\n102\t\n103\t- Rollback of LiteLLM config (LiteLLM is innocent here)\n104\t- Cache change in `ai-description` (lead 1)\n105\t- Scale-up of postgresql without addressing the source of load\n106\t- LLM model swap\n107\t\n108\t### 2.5 Deliberate simplifications\n109\t\n110\t- No second plugin for cross-plugin race (no `auto-describe`, no `OverlapDetection`)\n111\t- No Langfuse as a separate MCP (LiteLLM metrics live in `aj-metrics`)\n112\t- Static scenario — no dynamic patcher (seed loaded at container start, no time-based mutations)\n113\t\n114\t### 2.6 What KG changes — concrete traversals\n115\t\n116\t**With KG (~9 MCP calls to root cause + ownership):**\n117\t\n118\t1. `find_nodes(Service, {name: \"ai-description\"})` → node_id\n119\t2. `get_neighbors(ai-description, \"uses_datastore\", \"out\")` → `[postgresql-primary]`\n120\t3. `get_neighbors(postgresql-primary, \"uses_datastore\", \"in\")` → **`[ai-description, warehouse, host-app]`** (shared!)\n121\t4. For each: `aj-metrics.query_metric(service, \"db_query_rate\", window=incident)` → spike for `warehouse`\n122\t5. `find_nodes(Revision, {target: \"warehouse*\", window: \"4h\"})` → FeatureGate toggle authored by `p.warehouse`\n123\t6. `get_neighbors(warehouse, \"owned_by\")` → Team → escalation\n124\t\n125\t**Without KG (~15+ MCP calls in the optimal path):**\n126\t\n127\t1. `aj-metrics` for ai-description latency + LiteLLM latency (lead 2 distracts)\n128\t2. `aj-traces` for ai-description — spans against DB are long\n129\t3. `aj-catalog.find_service(\"ai-description\")` → `owner`, `uses_datastore_names: [\"postgresql-primary\"]`\n130\t4. **Decision: ask who else uses this DB** (tacit knowledge — the critical moment)\n131\t5. `aj-catalog.list_services()` → iterate, check `uses_datastore_names` for each\n132\t6. For each candidate: `aj-metrics.query_metric(..., \"db_query_rate\")`\n133\t7. Finds `warehouse`, asks `aj-changes.list_revisions(target=\"warehouse*\")`\n134\t8. Sees FeatureGate, `aj-catalog.find_service(\"warehouse\")` → owner\n135\t9. `aj-catalog.list_teams()` / `find_team(...)` → members\n136\t\n137\t### 2.7 Design decision: no inverse lookup in `aj-catalog`\n138\t\n139\t`aj-catalog` does NOT expose `list_services_using_datastore(name)`. Rationale: real catalogs (Backstage) don't have this primitive either — inverse lookups are flat. KG's uplift comes exactly from this asymmetry (KG can traverse with `direction=\"in\"`, catalog cannot). Shortening the non-KG path would give away the experiment.\n140\t\n141\t## 3. Ontology: Operational Topology Graph (OTG)\n142\t\n143\t### 3.1 Philosophy\n144\t\n145\t- **Single ontology**, not layered. All entities live in one Neo4j graph.\n146\t- **Custom names**, no leakage of inspiration sources (no \"Backstage\", \"OTel\", \"Langfuse\" in labels or attributes).\n147\t- Graph holds **topology + change history**. Time-series, logs, and spans live elsewhere (in `aj-metrics` / `aj-traces`).\n148\t- **Zero conclusion-attributes.** Nothing like `suspicious=true`, `is_culprit`, `bug_risk`. All attributes are neutral observations a real platform catalog would maintain.\n149\t\n150\t### 3.2 Node labels (8 types)\n151\t\n152\t| Label | Meaning | Attributes |\n153\t|---|---|---|\n154\t| **Service** | Long-running process delivering a platform function | `name` (unique), `tier` (`core`/`extension`/`integration`), `runtime` (`jvm`/`node`/`python`), `release_channel` (`stable`/`beta`), `lifecycle` (`production`/`experimental`/`deprecated`) |\n155\t| **Datastore** | Stateful dependency (DB, cache, queue, bucket) | `name` (unique), `kind` (`relational`/`keyvalue`/`object`/`stream`), `capacity_tier` (`shared`/`dedicated`) |\n156\t| **Endpoint** | Callable operation published by a Service | `path`, `method`, `version` |\n157\t| **Dependency** | External third-party consumed by a Service | `name` (unique), `vendor` (`internal`/`external`), `kind` (`saas`/`library_service`/`proxy`) |\n158\t| **FeatureGate** | Feature flag in the control plane | `name` (unique), `state` (`on`/`off`/`rollout`), `description` |\n159\t| **Revision** | Point of state change (deploy, config, gate flip) | `id` (unique), `target_kind` (`service`/`datastore`/`gate`/`dependency`), `target` (name), `kind` (`deploy`/`config`/`toggle`), `at` (ISO 8601), `summary` |\n160\t| **Team** | Group responsibility | `name` (unique), `kind` (`product`/`platform`/`partner`) |\n161\t| **Person** | Identified user account | `handle` (unique), `email` |\n162\t\n163\t### 3.3 Boundary decisions\n164\t\n165\t- **FeatureGate lives in the graph** (not only in `aj-changes`). It is a topological object (owned by, gates a service), not merely a change event. Its toggles are in Revisions; the gate itself is a reference point.\n166\t- **Revision lives in the graph** because it requires `affects` / `authored_by` edges. Full details (diff, long summary) returned by `aj-changes`; the graph holds id + short summary.\n167\t- **Person is in the graph** because ownership-escalation is 2-hop (`Service → Team ← Person`). Without it, KG loses a critical escalation path.\n168\t- **Metrics, logs, and spans are NOT in the graph.** Graph says \"Service X exists and uses Datastore Y\"; metrics say \"X-to-Y was 2000qpm yesterday.\"\n169\t\n170\t### 3.4 Edge types (8 relations, all directed)\n171\t\n172\t| Edge | From → To | Meaning | Cardinality |\n173\t|---|---|---|---|\n174\t| `owned_by` | Service / Datastore / Endpoint / Dependency / FeatureGate → Team | Which team is responsible | 1:1 (mandatory) |\n175\t| `member_of` | Person → Team | Person in team | N:1 |\n176\t| `exposes` | Service → Endpoint | Service publishes endpoint | 1:N |\n177\t| `uses_datastore` | Service → Datastore | Service reads/writes datastore | N:M |\n178\t| `uses_dependency` | Service → Dependency | Service calls external dep | N:M |\n179\t| `affects` | Revision → Service / Datastore / Dependency / FeatureGate | Revision changed this object | N:M |\n180\t| `authored_by` | Revision → Person | Who made the change | N:1 |\n181\t| `gates` | FeatureGate → Service | Which gate controls which services | N:M |\n182\t\n183\t**Intentional omissions:**\n184\t\n185\t- No `calls` / `depends_on_service` between Services. Service-to-service calls go through `exposes` → Endpoint, and actual invocations are in `aj-traces`.\n186\t- No `causes` / `blocks` / `impacts`. Such edges would be conclusions, not facts.\n187\t\n188\t### 3.5 Seed for the scenario\n189\t\n190\t**Services:**\n191\t\n192\t- `host-app` (tier=core, runtime=jvm, release_channel=stable, lifecycle=production)\n193\t- `ai-description` (tier=extension, runtime=jvm, release_channel=stable, lifecycle=production)\n194\t- `warehouse` (tier=extension, runtime=node, release_channel=stable, {"type":"assistant","message":{"model":"claude-opus-4-6","id":"msg_0177uLbVtYJ9L21aLvcWJcdR","type":"message","role":"assistant","content":[{"type":"tool_use","id":"toolu_01JmSkQJwfGHeeFxpRzDMUag","name":"Write","input":{"file_path":"/app/INCIDENT-REPORT.md","content":"# Incident Report: ai-description p99 latency degradation\n\n**Date:** 2026-04-17\n**Duration:** ~11:50 UTC -- 14:00 UTC (ongoing at time of mitigation)\n**Severity:** High\n**Affected service:** ai-description (owner: ai-team, m.ai@example.com)\n**Root cause owner:** warehouse-product (p.warehouse@example.com)\n\n## Summary\n\nThe `ai-description` service experienced a sustained p99 latency degradation from a ~1800ms baseline to ~5900ms. The root cause was the `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` feature gate being enabled on the `warehouse` service at 10:00 UTC, which triggered a high-volume sync loop issuing ~1700 queries/minute against `postgresql-primary` -- a datastore shared with `ai-description`. The resulting DB contention caused ai-description's product queries to accumulate 2500-2700ms of DB wait time per request.\n\n## Timeline\n\n| Time (UTC)  | Event |\n|-------------|-------|\n| 10:00       | `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` feature gate set to `on` by p.warehouse@example.com (rev-001) |\n| 10:05       | Warehouse `db_query_rate` begins climbing: 42 qpm -> 182 qpm |\n| ~11:00      | Warehouse `db_query_rate` plateaus at ~1700 qpm (40x baseline) |\n| 11:50       | ai-description `latency_p99` begins climbing above baseline (2129ms vs 1800ms baseline) |\n| 12:40       | ai-description `latency_p99` reaches ~5600ms and plateaus |\n| 14:00       | Mitigation applied: `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` gate set to `off` |\n\n## Diagnosis\n\n### Evidence gathered\n\n1. **Trace analysis** (t-001, t-002): Slow ai-description requests spent 2500-2700ms in `db_wait_ms` on `GET /api/products/{id}`, while the LLM call to litellm-proxy remained at 1350-1380ms in span duration. DB wait was the dominant latency contributor.\n\n2. **Metrics -- warehouse `db_query_rate`**: Jumped from ~42 qpm (baseline) to ~1700 qpm immediately after the feature gate was enabled at 10:00 UTC, then plateaued. This represents a 40x increase in query volume against postgresql-primary.\n\n3. **Metrics -- ai-description `latency_p99_ms`**: Stable at ~1800ms until 11:50, then climbed steadily to ~5900ms over the next hour. The ~2-hour lag between the warehouse load spike and the ai-description degradation is consistent with progressive connection pool saturation and buffer pool thrashing.\n\n4. **Metrics -- ai-description `llm_call_latency_ms`**: Also degraded (p99: ~1000ms -> ~3900ms), consistent with secondary effects of overall system resource pressure.\n\n5. **Change log**: The only change in the incident window was `rev-001` -- the toggle of `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` at 10:00 UTC.\n\n6. **Shared dependency**: Both `ai-description` and `warehouse` use `postgresql-primary` (confirmed via service catalog). No other datastore or dependency is shared.\n\n### Causal chain\n\n```\nwarehouse-full-inventory-sync gate ON (10:00 UTC)\n  -> warehouse full sync loop activates\n    -> ~1700 queries/min against postgresql-primary (40x baseline)\n      -> DB connection pool saturation / query queueing\n        -> ai-description product queries wait 2500-2700ms on DB\n          -> ai-description p99 latency degrades to ~5900ms\n```\n\n## Mitigation\n\n**Action:** Disabled the `warehouse-full-inventory-sync` feature gate via `set_feature_gate_state(name=\"warehouse-full-inventory-sync\", state=\"off\")`.\n\n**Revision:** rev-auto-fa2976e1\n\nThis stops the degradation at its source by halting the warehouse sync loop that was saturating the shared database. This is not a symptomatic workaround -- it removes the root cause load.\n\n## Notification\n\nThe **warehouse-product** team (p.warehouse@example.com) is responsible for the root cause. The full-inventory-sync feature, when enabled, produces unsustainable query load against the shared postgresql-primary datastore. Before re-enabling this gate, the warehouse-product team should:\n\n1. Evaluate whether the sync can use batch queries instead of per-SKU individual queries.\n2. Consider rate-limiting or throttling the sync loop.\n3. Assess whether warehouse needs a dedicated datastore to avoid contention with other services on postgresql-primary.\n"},"caller":{"type":"direct"}}],"stop_reason":null,"stop_sequence":null,"stop_details":null,"usage":{"input_tokens":1,"cache_creation_input_tokens":257,"cache_read_input_tokens":43160,"cache_creation":{"ephemeral_5m_input_tokens":257,"ephemeral_1h_input_tokens":0},"output_tokens":8,"service_tier":"standard","inference_geo":"not_available"},"context_management":null},"parent_tool_use_id":null,"session_id":"612724dd-b992-4873-9073-7a6e2fb260a8","uuid":"db7387f3-697d-4e60-9de1-386034a45b85"}
